 At the outset, the Chairman welcomed the Members to the meeting of the Committee and apprised 
them of the agenda of the meeting i.e. 
xxx xxx xxx; and to hear the views of the Ministry of Home 
Affairs on the subject „Atrocities and Crimes against Women‟. He also informed the Committee that the 
Home Secretary has been invited to make a presentation on the subject but due to his prior engagement, 
will join the meeting later. 
 
2. xxx    xxx    xxx. 
(On arrival of witnesses) 
 
3. The Chairman welcomed Shri V.S.K. Kaumudi, Special Secretary and DG BPR&D and other 
senior officers of the Ministries of Home Affairs, Law and Justice, Electronics and Information 
Technology and Women & Child Development to the meeting of the Committee.  
4. 
The Chairman observed that the Atrocities and Crimes against Women is a highly sensitive subject 
and has been a key issue in recent years. The crimes against women shows a rising trend for the past five  
years and 33,356 and 32,033 cases of rape have been registered in 2018 and 2019. There has also been 
non-compliance of the provisions of the law with respect to the registration of FIR in some States/UTs. 
This situation is very disturbing and raises a serious question on the state of law and order all over the 
country. Therefore, the Committee has invited the representatives of the Ministry of Home Affairs to 
make a comprehensive presentation on the subject.  
 
5. The Special Secretary, MHA made a presentation and briefed the Committee about the initiatives 
taken by the Ministry to prevent crimes against women and to ensure the safety of women which included 
the commencement of a Woman Safety Division in the MHA, creation of a national database on sexual 
offenders for use by the law enforcement agencies, launch of cyber-crime reporting portal, notification of 
standardized procedures for forensic evidence collection for dealing with sexual assault cases, specialized 
training to investigative and medical  
 
 
 
xxx  Relates to other matters. 

126 
 
officers on forensic evidence, strengthening of forensic sciences capabilities in the country at na tional and 
state levels, issuance of advisories for the proper and effective implementation of the measures taken by 
the Governments.  
 
6. At this point, the Home Secretary joined the meeting and informed the Committee that Police and 
Public Order is a State subject .  Despite that, the Ministry of Home Affairs has taken multiple initiatives 
like Crime and Criminal Tracking Network System (CCTNS), Crime Multi-Agency Centre (Cri-MAC), e-
courts, e-prosecution, e-prisons, e-forensics, specialized training and advisories to the State Governments 
to ensure the safety of women all across the country, etc.   
7. The Chairman and Members of the Committee raised queries
  that inter-alia included under-
utilization of Nirbhaya Fund,  laws promulgated by States to curb crimes against women, State-wise data 
on crimes against women like rape, trafficking etc., 
data bank of the sexual offenders, crimes against sex 
workers and problem faced by them to approach the legal system for redressal of their grievances, low 
conviction rate in registered rape cases and killing of victims of rape/gang rape, the role of alcohol and 
other drugs abuse in the crimes against women specifically in rape cases, poor living condition of women 
prisoners, under-reporting of cases of crimes against women and delay in investigation and filing of report  
by the Police, non-initiation of action against the police officers and medical practitioners f or not 
collecting evidences in rape cases in time, 
details of any scientific study undertaken to ascertain the causes 
of rapes and crimes against women, number of police officers booked under the rape charges, etc. 
8. T
he Chairman and Members expressed their concern regarding the inadequate number of women 
constable and officers in the police stations, non-registration of FIRs in rape cases particularly in rural 
areas, increasing child pornography and gender abusive content on the internet, increase in women/ girl 
trafficking during COVID-19 Pandemic lockdown, violation of the rules/provision/ judgment relating to 
non-disclosure of the identity of the victims by the media, etc. 
 
9. The Chairman and Members suggested various measures to contain the incidents of crimes against 
women like setting up of a data bank of the sexual offenders to identify the possible offender  and the need 
to re-look at the age limit of the juvenile in rape cases, setting up a committee to review the condition of 
women prisoners in the country, setting up of a separate desk in the Police stations to handle the cases of 
atrocities against women, publicizing the laws, schemes and various measures taken by the Government in 
regional languages all across the country,  establishment of a monitoring system at the district level to 
ensure that the rape cases are being investigated properly and
 making gender sensitization an integral part 
of the school curriculum.  
 
10. The Chairman informed that in subsequent meetings, the Committee will have a briefing by the 
Ministry of Women and Child Development on the subject. The Committee will also hear Non-
Governmental Organisations (NGO‟s) connected with the subject and one State Government from every 
region of the country for broader consultation and in-depth examination of the various aspect of the 
subject. In this direction, detailed information from the Ministry of Law and Justice on the Fast Track 
Courts and Training will also be sought. 
11. The Chairman informed the Members that in the next meeting, the Committee will have a 
presentation by ITBP on the working condition of Jawans and officers of ITBP who have been posted at 
high altitudes and harsh location of Northern Ladakh and elsewhere in view of the approaching winter 
season.   
12.
  On other queries, the Ministries were asked to furnish written replies to the Secretariat within 10 
days.

14. The Committee, then, adjourned at 2:30 p.m.

   At the outset, the Chairman welcomed the Members of the Committee to the meeting and apprised 
them about the agenda for the meeting i.e. to hear the views of the Ministry of Women & Child 
Development (MoW&CD) and Delhi Police on the subject „Atrocities and Crimes against Women‟ so as 
to have a wider and in-depth understanding and examination of the subject.  
 
2.  The Chairman informed the Members that the Ministry of Women & Child Development is the 
nodal Ministry for the advancement of women and children. It formulates plans, policies/ programmes, 
guides and coordinates the efforts of both governmental and non-governmental organizations for the 
safety of women and children. Regarding Delhi Police, the Chairman informed that their Special Police 
Unit for Women and Children (SPUWAC) is working to safeguard the right of women and Children in the 
national Capital.  
                                     
(On arrival of witnesses) 
 
3. The Chairman welcomed Shri Ram Mohan Mishra, Secretary, Ministry of Women & Child 
Development, Shri S.N. Shrivastava, Commissioner of Police, Delhi and other Senior Officers of the 
Ministry and Delhi Police to the meeting of the Committee.  
4.  The Secretary, Ministry of Women & Child Development informed the Committee about the legislative 
measures, schemes and programmes, initiatives taken through Nirbhaya Fund, and other measures taken 
by them to protect women's rights and ensure their safety. 
5. The Member Secretary, National Commission for Woman apprised the Committee that the NCW 
investigates and examines all matters relating to constitutional safeguards for women, to look into 
complaints and take suo moto notice of matters relating to deprivation of women's rights and has a 24x7 
online complaint portal for crimes committed against women. NCW has also been working with different 
State Commissions and investigating the complaints throughout the country. 
6. The Commissioner of Police, Delhi made a comprehensive presentation that included the creation 
of Special Police Unit for Women and Children, trend of crimes against women in the national capital, 
creation of 24x7 help desks in all police stations, Himmat Plus app, women helpline numbers, pro-active 
measures taken to stop crime against women, training and sensitization of police personnel and other 
initiatives taken by them.  
 
7. The Chairman and Members raised some queries pertaining to the MoW&CD that inter-alia 
included reasons for under-utilization of Nirbhaya Fund, measures taken by the MoW&CD to stop the 
dowry system in the country, State-wise number of shelter homes for women in the country and number of 
cases of sexual harassment reported thereof, number of police stations in the country having women 

130 
 
officers, State-wise number of cases registered against child marriages, steps taken to prevent online 
trolling/ abuse of women on social media platforms, etc. 
8. Regarding Delhi Police, queries were raised by the Chairman and Members about the total number 
of cases of rape registered, guilty arrested, cases pending and conviction rate achieved thereon in De lhi, 
sensitization drives carried out by Delhi Police, co-ordination mechanism between Delhi Police, MHA and 
States bordering Delhi to tackle crimes in Delhi and NCR, steps taken to increase the representation of 
women in Delhi Police,  incentives given by Delhi Police to trace missing children, number of pa trolling 
vans that are manned by the female constables,  number of chain snatching cases, number of women who 
have used apps like Himmat App, etc. that are dedicated to the safety of women, number of operators 
handling women's helpline number, number of CCTV cameras installed and its effectiveness in preventing 
crime in Delhi, etc.
 
9. The Chairman and Members also made some suggestions to the MoW&CD and Delhi Police that 
inter-alia included creation of Inter-Ministerial Co-ordination Committee including Central and State 
Governments to tackle crime against women, study/research to be conducted by Bureau of Police Research 
and Development (BPR&D) to understand the pattern of crimes against women all across the country, 
permanent rehabilitation arrangements for  trafficked women/children by providing them shelter, food, 
education, employment, etc., one common helpline number instead of multiple numbers for women in 
distress, co-ordination of MoW&CD with civil society organizations to develop and establish good 
practices to eradicate violence against women, making gender sensitization an integral part of the school 
curriculum and conducting public awareness programmes through press and media in regional/ local 
languages to educate the public about laws, helpline numbers/ online portals dedicated for the safety and 
security of women. 
10. The Secretary, MoW&CD and Commissioner of Police, Delhi briefly responded to some queries 
and on other queries,  MoW&CD and Delhi Police was asked to furnish written replies to the Secretariat 
within 10 days.

At the outset, the Chairman welcomed the Members of the Committee to the meeting and apprised 
them of the agenda for the meeting i.e. to hear the views of the NGOs, namely, „All India Women‟s 
Conference‟ (AIWC), „Jagori‟ and „Action India‟ on the subject „Atrocities and Crimes against Women‟. 
2.  Thereafter, he informed that the NGOs are supplementing the efforts of the Government in 
women empowerment and in extending the benefits of the policies and schemes to the women. NGOs are 
also making women aware of their legal rights, helping them in becoming independent and motivated to 
take decisions in matters concerning them. Therefore, the three NGOs working in the matters related to 
progress, welfare, protection and empowerment of women have been called to present their views on the 
subject.  
                                   
(On arrival of witnesses) 
 
3
. The Chairman welcomed the senior officers of the Ministry of Home Affairs and Smt. Kuljit Kaur, 
Secretary-General, 'All India Women‟s Conference' (AIWC), Ms. Jayashree Velankar, Director, ' Jagori' 
and Ms. Gyanwati, Senior Paralegal, Mahila Panchayat Program, 'Action India' to the meeting of the 
Committee.  
4. The Chairman stated that the Committee would like to know from the representatives of the NGOs 
about their activities and programmes undertaken by them pertaining to the subject; the number of women 
who benefitted thereof, relief provided to destitute and poor women during COVID-19 lockdowns and 
research work done by them and also about the difficulties and obstacles being faced by the NGOs at the 
ground level while executing schemes/programmes and interventions that are required.
 
5. The Secretary- General, 'All India Women‟s Conference' (AIWC) made a comprehensive 
presentation before the Committee. She informed the Committee about the organisational structure of 
„AIWC‟, major steps taken by the Government including stringent laws, creation of Nirbhaya Fund, 
change in definition of rape, expansion of the number of Fast Track Courts, forensic labs for safety of 
women. She also highlighted the problems faced by victims of rape and sexual harassment, huge 
pendency of cases of crimes against women, low conviction rate, rise in cases of domestic violence during 
COVID-19 lockdowns, crimes against women in public transport like buses, etc., 
6. The Secretary-General, AIWC, then informed the Committee about the steps taken by AIWC for 
safety and security of women and to prevent crimes against them that inter-alia included establishment of 
short stay homes, rehabilitation including vocational training and counselling to victims of violence. Some 
suggestions were also made by her that included conducting a women safety week, increase in number of 
women judges, forensic labs, shelter homes and short stay homes near hospitals, gender sensitization 
drives, providing representation to the women working in field of women's safety in National and State 
Councils for Women, study of reasons for crimes against women by juveniles, establishment of 
reformation homes for counselling sexual offenders, etc. 
7.
 The Director, 'Jagori' apprised the Committee about the main reasons for violence against women, 
low participation of women in work force, issue of funding of women Self Help Groups (SHGs) 
particularly in rural areas, etc. She emphasized on certainty of conviction, compulsory registration of 

133 
 
cases of crimes against women, role of politicians in preventing crimes against women, violence against 
women belonging to SC/ST community, effective implementation of the Sexual Harassment of Women at 
Workplace (Prevention, Prohibition and Redressal) Act, 2013, etc.  
8. The Director, 'Jagori' also made suggestions to the Committee about a Pan-India common national 
helpline number, facilities for transgender community, time -bound procedure for rescue, rehabilitati on 
and repatriation of victims of trafficking, decriminalization of sex workers under Immoral Traffic 
(Prevention) Act, 1956, increase in budgetary allocation on schemes for protection of women, increase in 
number of shelter homes and one stop centres, synergy between Women and Children Department and 
Health Department along with training programmes for health staff, safety of women in public transports 
along with regular safety audits, adoption of draft National Policy for Women, 2016, ending the practice  
of witch-hunting and witch-branding, etc. Ratification of convention number 190 of the International 
Labour Organization (ILO) by India, which is about sexual harassment at workplace and involvement of 
district-level legal volunteers to provide legal help to women. 
9.
 The Senior Paralegal, Mahila Panchayat Program, 'Action India' briefed the Committee about the 
work done by the organisation in addressing domestic violence, reproductive health rights, water & 
sanitation and safety & security of women. She particularly mentioned their programme „ Mahila 
Panchayat’, which works for preventing domestic violence in resettlement colonies in Delhi.  She also 
made some suggestions that included proper medical attention and counselling with financial assistance to 
the victims of violence and also their family for rehabilitation, effective inter-State co-ordination among st 
investigating and law enforcement agencies to ensure that the convicts do not flee away across State 
borders after committing crime. Involvement of NGOs with feminist outlook in filing of charge sheets and 
extending help to the victims during the process of investigation, and creation of a national-level 
committee comprising of experts on legal, psychology, sociology and economic fields to analyze the 
reasons for cause of crime against women and reasons for delay in justice and suggested for ef fective, 
efficient and timely justice.   
10.
 The Chairman and Members raised some queries that inter-alia included reasons for confinement 
of NGOs working in field of women's safety to the urban areas, reasons for incidents of majority of ra pe 
case by known persons, trafficking of women and girls, discrimination faced by transgender community, 
problems of sex workers, role of society to prevent crimes against women, increasing cyber-crimes against 
women, poor implementation of women related laws, low representation of women in State Police force, 
etc. 
11. The Chairman and Members also made some suggestions that inter-alia included involvement of 
Panchayats and NGOs in community policing, creation of national awareness led by Prime Minister and  
Chief Ministers regarding safety and security of women in the country, extending legitimate rights and  
entitlements to sex workers and also to transgender community including creation of separate washroom 
facilities for transgender at all public places, leadership of politicians in gender sensitization, strengthening 
of criminal justice system, etc. 
 
12. The Committee decided to hear the views of at least one national level organization of Scheduled 
Caste (SC) and Scheduled Tribe (ST) in the subsequent meetings on the subject. 
13.
 The NGOs briefly responded to some queries and on other queries, they were asked to furnish 
written replies to the Secretariat within 10 days.

3.  The Chairman informed that 'Police' and 'Public Order' are State subjects under the Seventh 
Schedule to the Constitution of India. The responsibility to maintain law and order, the safety of women 
and children and the prevention of crimes against them lies with the respective State Governments. 
Therefore, to gain the perspective and efforts of the State Governments in ensuring the safety and s ecurity 
of women and children in States and to prevent crimes against them, the Committee has invited the 
representatives of the State Governments of Rajasthan and Uttar Pradesh.

of Uttar Pradesh. He also welcomed Smt. Saheli Ghosh Roy, Joint Secretary, Ministry of Home Affairs 
and Senior Officers of the Ministry and State Governments to the meeting of the Committee. 
5. The representatives of the State Government of Rajasthan informed the Committee about the 
reasons for the increase in crimes against women and children; establishment of Special Investigation Unit 
for crimes against women; free registration of FIRs and its monitoring through decoy operations; self-
defense training to women; Mahila Police Patrolling Units; establishment of women help desks, helpline 
number; RajCop Citizen App; installation of CCTVs; percentage and reservation of women in Police; 
awareness campaigns through social media; shelter homes and One-stop Centres; creation of Samudaeek 
Sampark Samooh,  (community liasoning group) to promote community policing; inclusion of gender 
sensitization as a part of the school curriculum; etc. 
6.  The representatives of the State Government of Uttar Pradesh informed the Committee about the 
State helpline numbers; reasons for the increase in the number of crimes against women; formation of 
Anti-Romeo Squads; monitoring of hotspot areas, increase in conviction rate; campaigns conducted under 
Mission Shakti; self-defense workshops; safety of women in public transport; women help desks; 
implementation of advisories of MHA; no provision of zero FIR; utilization of Nirbhaya Fund; initiatives 
under safe city project in Lucknow ; formation of women Police Response Vehicles (PRVs); legal 
awareness campaigns; inclusion of gender sensitization as a part of the school curriculum; etc. 

140 
 
7.  The Chairman and Members raised some queries pertaining to the State Government of Rajasthan 
and Uttar Pradesh that inter-alia included a number of Fast Track Courts and a number of charge-sheets 
filed thereon; reasons for low conviction rate; Safe City Project; allocation and utilization of Nirbhaya 
Fund; legal awareness campaigns; condition of shelter homes; registration of false cases; decoy 
operations; management of shelter homes; effectiveness of Anti-Romeo Squad; mechanism of Zero-FIR; 
survey to check the use of helpline numbers by poor and destitute women; mechanism of interlinking with 
other Departments, etc. 
8.  The Chairman and Members also made some suggestions to the State Governments of Rajasthan 
and Uttar Pradesh that inter-alia included the creation of a pan- India single 3-digit helpline number; 
increase of police stations in Rajasthan and Uttar Pradesh; increase in the number of decoy opera tions; 
enhancement in representation of women in Police, taking strict action against the person lodging false 
complaint; installation of a complaint box in all schools; devising inter-Ministerial co-ordination 
mechanism to tackle crimes against women and children; etc. 
9.  The representatives of the State Governments of Rajasthan and Uttar Pradesh briefly responded to 
some queries and on other queries, they were asked to furnish written replies to the Secretariat within ten 
days. 
(Witnesses withdrew) 
10. A Verbatim record of the proceeding of the meeting was kept. 
11. The Committee, then, adjourned at 02:09 P.M.

1. Shri Alapan Bandyopadhyay, Chief Secretary

3. Smt. Sanghamitra Ghosh, Secretary (Women & Child Development and Social Welfare

4. Shri Virendra, Director General & Inspector General of Police 
5. Shri Peeyush Pandey, Addl. Director-General & Inspector General of Police (Correctional

6. Shri Muralidhar Sharma, Joint Commissioner of Police, Crime, Kolkata Police. 
 
 At the outset, the Chairman welcomed the Members of the Committee to the meeting and apprised 
them about the agenda for the meeting i.e. to hear the views of the State Governments of Haryana and 
West Bengal on the subject „ Atrocities and Crimes against Wom en & Children‟ and Ministry of Home 
Affairs on issues pertaining to cyber crimes involving women & children as part of the subject under 
consideration. 
(On arrival of witnesses) 
2.    The Chairman welcomed Shri Vijai Vardhan, Chief Secretary, Shri Manoj Yadava, Director-Ge neral 
of Police of the State Government of Haryana, Shri Alapan Bandyopadhyay, Chief Sec retary, Shri 
Virendra, Director-General & Inspector-General of Police of the State Government of West Benga l and 
Senior Officers of the Ministry of Home Affairs to the meeting of the Committee.   
3.   The Chief Secretary of Haryana informed the Committee about the initiatives taken by the State 
Government to deal with atrocities and crimes against women; to improve sex ratio in the State; setting up 
of shelter homes and special courts for crimes against women and children; etc. 
4. The Director-General of Police, State Government of Haryana highlighted the reasons for increase  
in percentage of crimes against women in the State which inter-alia includes free registration of cases; 
establishment of all-women police stations; low conviction rate; training courses for the police personnel; 
initiatives taken under the Scheme of Cyber Crime Prevention against Women and Children (CCPWC); 
etc. 
5. The Chairman and Members raised some queries pertaining to the State Governments of Haryana 
that inter-alia included Mahila Police Volunteer Scheme; reasons for under-utilization of Nirbhaya fund; 
reasons for low number of distress calls on the Durga Shakti App; community policing and conducting 
survey to compare all-women Police Stations vis-a-vis traditional Police Stations. Whether any study has 
been conducted to understand the reasons for crimes against women; etc. 
6. The Chairman and Members of the Committee gave some suggestions that included how to 
promote community policing involving women organizations and Panchayati Raj Institutions (PRI); 
inclusion of gender sensitization in the educational curriculum at an early age; a national study should be 

143 
 
conducted to check the efficacy of stringent provisions in laws to curb crimes against women and 
children; etc. 
7. The Chief Secretary of the State of West Bengal apprised the Committee about the various 
initiatives taken to curb crimes against women which inter-alia included; setting up of all-women police 
stations; setting up of cyber police station in all police districts, increasing the strength of women police  
personnel;  women helpline numbers; dedicated help desks for women; progress made in the 
establishment of fast track special courts; reasons for low conviction rate in cases of crimes against 
women; socio-cultural welfare schemes for empowering women; etc.   
 8. The Chairman and the Members raised some queries that included progress made in setting up of 
fast track courts; incidents of child marriages and trafficking of women and children in the State; impac t 
of social schemes in empowering women; etc. 
9. The representatives of the Ministry of Home Affairs apprised the Committee about the growing 
prevalence of cyber crimes especially against women and children; two Schemes of the Ministry of Home  
Affairs for prevention of cyber crimes i.e. Cyber Crime Prevention against Women and Children and 
Indian Cyber Crime Coordination Centre Scheme; establishment of National Cyber Forensic Laboratory at 
Dwarka, New Delhi; ongoing research projects; cyber crime awareness campaign through e lectronic and 
digital media, training modules on cyber crimes; etc.   
10. The Chairman and the Members raised some queries that inter-alia included challenges associated 
with cyber crimes including jurisdictional complexity; allocation of funds to curb cyber crimes; cyber 
helpline number; mechanism available to block pornographic content from the internet; specialized 
training of police personnel, public prosecutors and judicial officers on various aspect of cyber crimes; 
establishment of cyber forensic and training laboratories in States; creation of a separate Ministry to deal 
with cyber crimes; etc. 
11. The Chairman and the Members of the Committee gave some suggestions that inter-alia included 
a mechanism for inter-State, inter-agency coordination and optimum utilization of existing resources, 
creation of a  nodal body to deal with cyber crimes; tracing, tracking and blocking of unauthoriz ed Virtual 
Private Networks (VPN) and dark web, regular interaction of administration with social media companies 
to check propaganda on their platforms, signing of pacts with other countries to tackle trans-national cyber 
crimes, etc. 
12. The representatives of State Governments of Haryana, West Bengal and the Ministry of Home 
Affairs briefly responded to some queries and on other queries, they were asked to furnish written replies 
to the Secretariat within 10 days. 
                                                    (Witnesses withdrew) 
13. A Verbatim record of the proceeding of the meeting was kept. 
14. The Committee, then, adjourned at 01:45 p.m.
